=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Given an integer array nums, find the number of subsequences of size 5 of nums with a unique middle mode.
Since the answer may be very large, return it modulo 10^9 + 7.
A mode of a sequence of numbers is defined as the element that appears the maximum number of times in the sequence.
A sequence of numbers contains a unique mode if it has only one mode.
A sequence of numbers seq of size 5 contains a unique middle mode if the middle element (seq[2]) is a unique mode.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,1,1,1,1,1]
Output: 6
Explanation:
[1, 1, 1, 1, 1] is the only subsequence of size 5 that can be formed, and it has a unique middle mode of 1. This subsequence can be formed in 6 different ways, so the output is 6. 

Example 2:

Input: nums = [1,2,2,3,3,4]
Output: 4
Explanation:
[1, 2, 2, 3, 4] and [1, 2, 3, 3, 4] each have a unique middle mode because the number at index 2 has the greatest frequency in the subsequence. [1, 2, 2, 3, 3] does not have a unique middle mode because 2 and 3 appear twice.

Example 3:

Input: nums = [0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8]
Output: 0
Explanation:
There is no subsequence of length 5 with a unique middle mode.

 
Constraints:

5 <= nums.length <= 1000
-10^9 <= nums[i] <= 10^9

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def subsequencesWithMiddleMode(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Implementation details:
```
MOD = 10**9+7
def c2(k):
    return k*(k-1)//2 if k>=2 else 0
```
Inside loop:
```
total = c2(p) * c2(n-p-1)
A = pref[x]; B = suff[x]
P = p - A
S = (n - p - 1) - B
sum_c_pref = 0
sum_c_suff = 0
T1 = T2 = T3 = 0
for v in range(m):
    if v == x: continue
    a = pref[v]; b = suff[v]
    if a >= 2: sum_c_pref += a*(a-1)//2
    if b >= 2: sum_c_suff += b*(b-1)//2
    ab = a*b
    T1 += ab
    T2 += a*b*b
    T3 += a*a*b
D_pref = c2(P) - sum_c_pref
D_suff = c2(S) - sum_c_suff
L0 = c2(P)
R0 = c2(S)
L1 = A * P
R1 = B * S
invalid0 = L0 * R0
invalid1 = L1 * R0 + L0 * R1
D1 = P * D_suff - S * T1 + T2
D2 = S * D_pref - P * T1 + T3
valid = total - invalid0 - invalid1 + D1 + D2
ans = (ans + valid) % MOD
```

Let's perhaps derive a more optimized O(n*m) but with arrays. We can maintain suffix counts and prefix counts. Need compress values:
```
vals = sorted(set(nums))
idx = {v:i for i,v in enumerate(vals)}
arr = [idx[v] for v in nums]
m = len(vals)
pref = [0]*m
suff = [0]*m
for v in arr: suff[v]+=1
```
Then loop:
```
ans=0
for p, x in enumerate(arr):
    suff[x]-=1
    if p >= 2 and n-p-1 >= 2:
        A = pref[x]
        B = suff[x]
        P = p - A
        S = n - p - 1 - B
        total = comb2(p) * comb2(n-p-1)
        sum_c_pref = 0
        sum_c_suff = 0
        T1 = T2 = T3 = 0
        for v in range(m):
            if v == x: continue
            a = pref[v]
            b = suff[v]
            if a >= 2:
                sum_c_pref += a*(a-1)//2
            if b >= 2:
                sum_c_suff += b*(b-1)//2
            ab = a*b
            T1 += ab
            T2 += a*b*b
            T3 += a*a*b
        D_pref = comb2(P) - sum_c_pref
        D_suff = comb2(S) - sum_c_suff
        L0 = comb2(P)
        R0 = comb2(S)
        L1 = A * P
        R1 = B * S
        invalid0 = L0 * R0
        invalid1 = L1 * R0 + L0 * R1
        D1 = A * (P * D_suff - S * T1 + T2)
        D2 = B * (S * D_pref - P * T1 + T3)
        valid = total - invalid0 - invalid1 + D1 + D2
        ans = (ans + valid) % MOD
    pref[x]+=1
return ans % MOD
```
Need consider p range: If p<2 or n-p-1<2, total=0; skip. But if skip, D not computed. Fine. However if p>=2 but n-p-1<2? skip. If p<2 but n-p-1>=2? skip. Good.

Potential issue: We update suff[x]-=1 before computing. If p<2 and skip, still suff correct for next? At p=0, suff decremented current, then pref incremented. At p=1, suff decremented current, etc. Good.

Potential issue in D1/D2 version: D_pref and D_suff are computed as C2[P] - sum_c_pref. If P=0, C2[0]=0, sum_c_pref=0, D_pref=0. Good.
Need compute sum_c_pref/suff in loop. We can compute T1/T2/T3. Then:
```
D_pref = C2[P] - sum_c_pref
D_suff = C2[S] - sum_c_suff
L0 = C2[P]
R0 = C2[S]

R1 = B * S
invalid0 = L0 * R0

D1 = A * (P * D_suff - S * T1 + T2)
D2 = B * (S * D_pref - P * T1 + T3)
valid = total - invalid0 - invalid1 + D1 + D2
```
Let's test with T1 nonzero example [9,1,2,9,1,3]? Actually n=7 p=3, A=1,B=1,P=2,S=2. We computed valid6. Let's run D version:

invalid0=1, invalid1=2*1 +1*2=4.
D1=A*(P*D_suff - S*T1 + T2)=1*(2*1 -2*1 +1)=1.
D2=B*(S*D_pref - P*T1 + T3)=1*(2*1 -2*1 +1)=1.
valid=9-1-4+1+1=6. Good.

Let's draft final code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def subsequencesWithMiddleMode(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        n = len(nums)
        vals = sorted(set(nums))
        comp = {v:i for i,v in enumerate(vals)}
        arr = [comp[v] for v in nums]
        m = len(vals)
        
        C2 = [i*(i-1)//2 for i in range(n+1)]
        
        pref = [0]*m
        suff = [0]*m
        for v in arr:
            suff[v] += 1
            
        ans = 0
        for p, x in enumerate(arr):
            suff[x] -= 1
            
            if p >= 2 and n - p - 1 >= 2:
                A = pref[x]
                B = suff[x]
                P = p - A          # non-x before
                S = (n - p - 1) - B  # non-x after
                
                total = C2[p] * C2[n-p-1]
                
                sum_c_pref = 0
                sum_c_suff = 0
                T1 = T2 = T3 = 0
                
                for v in range(m):
                    if v == x:
                        continue
                    a = pref[v]
                    b = suff[v]
                    sum_c_pref += C2[a]
                    sum_c_suff += C2[b]
                    ab = a * b
                    T1 += ab
                    T2 += ab * b
                    T3 += ab * a
                
                D_pref = C2[P] - sum_c_pref
                D_suff = C2[S] - sum_c_suff
                
                L0 = C2[P]
                R0 = C2[S]
                L1 = A * P
                R1 = B * S
                
                invalid0 = L0 * R0
                invalid1 = L1 * R0 + L0 * R1
                
                D1 = A * (P * D_suff - S * T1 + T2)
                D2 = B * (S * D_pref - P * T1 + T3)
                
                valid = total - invalid0 - invalid1 + D1 + D2
                ans = (ans + valid) % MOD
            
            pref[x] += 1
            
        return ans % MOD
```

Need check `T2 += ab * b` equals a*b*b. `T3 += ab * a` equals a*a*b. Good.

Potential issue: `C2[a]` where a can be 0; C2[0]=0. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def subsequencesWithMiddleMode(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        n = len(nums)

        # Coordinate compression
        vals = sorted(set(nums))
        comp = {v: i for i, v in enumerate(vals)}
        arr = [comp[v] for v in nums]
        m = len(vals)

        # C2[k] = k choose 2
        C2 = [i * (i - 1) // 2 for i in range(n + 1)]

        pref = [0] * m
        suff = [0] * m
        for v in arr:
            suff[v] += 1

        ans = 0

        for p, x in enumerate(arr):
            # Current element is the middle element of the subsequence.
            # Remove it from suffix so suff contains only elements after p.
            suff[x] -= 1

            if p >= 2 and n - p - 1 >= 2:
                A = pref[x]              # x's before p
                B = suff[x]              # x's after p
                P = p - A                # non-x before p
                S = (n - p - 1) - B      # non-x after p

                total = C2[p] * C2[n - p - 1]

                sum_c_pref = 0
                sum_c_suff = 0
                T1 = T2 = T3 = 0

                for v in range(m):
                    if v == x:
                        continue
                    a = pref[v]
                    b = suff[v]

                    sum_c_pref += C2[a]
                    sum_c_suff += C2[b]

                    ab = a * b
                    T1 += ab
                    T2 += ab * b
                    T3 += ab * a

                # Pairs of two different non-x values in prefix/suffix
                D_pref = C2[P] - sum_c_pref
                D_suff = C2[S] - sum_c_suff

                # Left/right pair types with respect to x
                L0 = C2[P]          # no x in left pair
                R0 = C2[S]          # no x in right pair
                L1 = A * P          # exactly one x in left pair
                R1 = B * S          # exactly one x in right pair

                # Invalid: no outer x, or exactly one outer x.
                invalid0 = L0 * R0
                invalid1 = L1 * R0 + L0 * R1

                # Among exactly-one-outer-x cases, add back those where
                # the three non-x elements are all distinct.
                D1 = A * (P * D_suff - S * T1 + T2)
                D2 = B * (S * D_pref - P * T1 + T3)

                valid = total - invalid0 - invalid1 + D1 + D2
                ans = (ans + valid) % MOD

            pref[x] += 1

        return ans % MOD
```
